Download data as CSV or manually copy from the web interface\n2. Organize by:\n   - **Column A**: Indicator names\n   - **Column B**: Units (% of GDP, index, etc.)\n   - **Columns C onwards**: Years (2018, 2019, ... 2043)\n3. Include both historical (actual) and projected data\n\n## Calculating Missing Values in Excel\n\n### GDP Deflator Projection\nWhen historical GDP deflator is available:\n1. Calculate growth rate: `=(Current_Year - Previous_Year) / Previous_Year`\n2. Average recent 4 years of growth: `=AVERAGE(Range_of_4_growth_rates)`\n3. Project forward: `=Prior_Year_Value * (1 + Fixed_Growth_Rate)`\n\n### Example Formula Structure\n```\nHistorical: Cell contains actual value\nProjection Start: =Prior_Year * (1 + AVERAGE($Historical_Growth_Range))\nSubsequent Years: =Prior_Year * (1 + AVERAGE($Historical_Growth_Range))\n```\n\n## Common Issues\n- **Missing data**: Use IMF regional aggregates or peer countries\n- **Unit inconsistencies**: Ensure all values use same units (e.g., all in USD billions)\n- **Projection periods**: Typically extend 5-10 years beyond latest actual data\n\n## Best Practices\n- Document data sources and extraction dates\n- Keep both nominal and real values if available\n- Flag estimated vs. actual data\n- Maintain Excel formulas (don't hardcode projected values)\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":997,"cache_read_input_tokens":18094,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":997,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0e7df011-350c-4ba1-a91f-e7f41ee03588","uuid":"9e01fbae-1a3a-4c8a-83e3-5fba123efe9f"}

Download data as CSV or manually copy from the web interface\n2. Organize by:\n   - **Column A**: Indicator names\n   - **Column B**: Units (% of GDP, index, etc.)\n   - **Columns C onwards**: Years (2018, 2019, ... 2043)\n3. Include both historical (actual) and projected data\n\n## Calculating Missing Values in Excel\n\n### GDP Deflator Projection\nWhen historical GDP deflator is available:\n1. Project forward: `=Prior_Year_Value * (1 + Fixed_Growth_Rate)`\n\n### Example Formula Structure\n```\nHistorical: Cell contains actual value\nProjection Start: =Prior_Year * (1 + AVERAGE($Historical_Growth_Range))\nSubsequent Years: =Prior_Year * (1 + AVERAGE($Historical_Growth_Range))\n```\n\n## Common Issues\n- **Missing data**: Use IMF regional aggregates or peer countries\n- **Unit inconsistencies**: Ensure all values use same units (e.g., all in USD billions)\n- **Projection periods**: Typically extend 5-10 years beyond latest actual data\n\n## Best Practices\n- Document data sources and extraction dates\n- Keep both nominal and real values if available\n- Flag estimated vs. actual data\n- Maintain Excel formulas (don't hardcode projected values)\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_014GKYyEbY283a5NRBKmhJCv","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01UJp4Qc2ewTb5PptzTkyLXH","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/supply-use-table-analysis.md","content":"# Supply and Use Table (SUT) Analysis in Excel\n\n## Overview\nSupply and Use Tables are the foundational framework for input-output analysis and national accounting. This skill covers extracting, integrating, and analyzing SUT data in Excel.\n\n## Structure of Supply and Use Tables\n\n### Supply Table (Product x Industry)\n- **Rows**: Products (goods and services)\n- **Columns**: Industries (production sectors)\n- **Cells**: Domestic production of products by each industry\n- **Additional columns**: Imports, Margins, Taxes\n\n### Use Table (Product x Industry)\n- **Rows**: Products (goods and services)\n- **Columns**: Industries (users/consumers) + Final Demand categories\n- **Cells**: Quantity of each product used by each industry/sector\n- **Final Demand**: Household consumption, government, investment, exports\n\n### Key Calculations from SUT\n\n#### Import Content Share\nThis measures the proportion of imported inputs in total supply:\n\n```\nImport_Content_Share = Total_Imports / (Domestic_Production + Total_Imports)\n\nOr for specific product:\n= Imported_Product / (Domestic_Product + Imported_Product)\n```\n\n#### Total Supply\n```\nTotal_Supply = Domestic_Production + Imports - Margins + Taxes_on_Products\n```\n\n#### Total Use\n```\nTotal_Use_by_Industry = Intermediate_Use + Value_Added\n```\n\n## Integrating SUT into National Accounting\n\n### Linking SUT to Demand-Side Framework\n1. **Total Imports from Supply Table**: Used in import content calculations\n2. **Final Demand by Product**: Breakdown of consumption, investment, exports\n3. **Industry Margins and Taxes**: Adjustments from basic prices to purchaser prices\n\n### File Organization in Excel\n- **Sheet \"SUPPLY\"**: Original supply table (Product x Industry)\n- **Sheet \"USE\"**: Original use table (Product x Industry + Final Demand)\n- **Sheet \"SUT Calc\"**: Derived calculations and import content shares\n  - Column C: Domestic Supply\n  - Column D: Imports\n  - Column E: Total Supply (or use for import content calculations)\n  - Columns F-H: Additional derived metrics\n\n## Import Content Share Calculation\n\nFor demand-side shock analysis:\n```\nCell C46 (Import Content Share) = Total_Imports / Total_Supply\n\nIn Excel:\n=SUM(Imported_products_range) / SUM(Total_supply_range)\n```\n\n### Application to Investment Shock\n- Higher import content share = larger import effect from investment shock\n- Used to calibrate multiplier effects and trade balance impacts\n\n## Working with Geostat (Georgian Statistical Office)\n\n### Data Access\n- URL: https://www.geostat.ge/en\n- Look for \"Supply and Use Tables\" or \"Input-Output Tables\"\n- Typically published in Excel format\n- Usually at product level 38x38 or similar matrix size\n\n### File Handling\n1. Download original file\n2. Copy SUPPLY sheet as-is (preserve original names)\n3. Copy USE sheet as-is (preserve original names)\n4. Create separate \"SUT Calc\" sheet for calculations\n5. Link to original sheets with formulas (don't copy values)\n\n## Best Practices\n- Always link to source sheets, never hardcode values\n- Maintain product classifications (HS codes or local classification)\n- Document data year and revision status\n- Keep import and domestic values separate\n- Verify row and column totals as controls\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":872,"cache_read_input_tokens":19091,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":872,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0e7df011-350c-4ba1-a91f-e7f41ee03588","uuid":"423347db-571b-4082-9676-1f0551510dd4"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01X9uT2ubghEjzjwSNt9jEMo","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01UceoEdjZu1cPMBEZ6Df8Fn","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/demand-side-macro-accounting.md","content":"# Demand-Side Macroeconomic Accounting Framework for Small Open Economies\n\n## Overview\nThis skill covers building demand-side national accounting frameworks (National Accounts) to analyze shocks like investment spending in small open economies using the macro accounting identity.\n\n## Core National Accounting Identity\n\nFor a small open economy:\n```\nY = C + I + G + (X - M)\n\nWhere:\nY = GDP (output/income)\nC = Private consumption\nI = Private investment\nG = Government consumption\nX = Exports\nM = Imports\n```\n\nOr rearranged:\n```\nGDP = Final Demand - Import Content of Demand\n```\n\n## Demand Multiplier Concept\n\n### Definition\nThe multiplier measures how much GDP increases for each unit of autonomous demand increase:\n```\nGDP_Change = Multiplier × Demand_Shock\n```\n\n### Multiplier Values by Economy Type\n- **Closed economy**: 1.0 to 2.5 (higher due to limited import leakage)\n- **Small open economy**: 0.5 to 1.0 (lower due to import substitution)\n- **Georgia example**: 0.8 (typical for small emerging market)\n\n### Multiplier Assumptions\n- **Demand Multiplier = 0.8**: For Georgia baseline scenario\n- **Demand Multiplier = 1.0**: For less open economy scenario\n- Based on import content share and consumption patterns\n\n## Investment Shock Analysis\n\n### Setup\n- **Shock Size**: $6.5 billion USD\n- **Duration**: 8 years (2026-2033)\n- **Shape**: Bell-shaped distribution (peaks mid-period)\n\n### Bell-Shaped Distribution Formula\n```\nYear_Share = (1 - ABS(Year_Relative_Position - Mid_Point) / Half_Period)^2\n\nExample for 8-year period:\n- Year 1: 25% of total\n- Years 2-3: 95-100% of total\n- Year 4-5: 100% of total (peak)\n- Year 6-7: 95% of total\n- Year 8: 25% of total\n```\n\n### Calculating Annual Investment Values\n```\nAnnual_Investment_USD = Total_Shock × Year_Share\nAnnual_Investment_Local = Annual_Investment_USD × Exchange_Rate (Lari/USD)\n```\n\n## Price Adjustments: Nominal vs. Real\n\n### Using GDP Deflator\n```\nInvestment_Deflator = GDP_Deflator (given in skill)\nNominal_Investment = Real_Investment × Investment_Deflator\n```\n\n### Deflator Index\n- Base year typically = 100\n- Subsequent years indexed relative to base\n- Growth rate = (Current - Previous) / Previous\n\n## Scenario Analysis Structure\n\n### Sheet Organization\n```\nSheet \"NA\" (National Accounts) contains:\n\nScenario 1: Baseline\n- Demand Multiplier = 0.8\n- Import Content Share = (from SUT_Calc, Cell C46)\n- Bell-shaped investment profile\n\nScenario 2: Alternative (Lower Import Leakage)\n- Demand Multiplier = 1.0\n- Import Content Share = (from SUT_Calc, Cell C46)\n- Same investment profile\n\nScenario 3: Alternative (Higher Imports)\n- Demand Multiplier = 0.8\n- Import Content Share = 0.5\n- Same investment profile\n```\n\n## Excel Implementation: National Accounts Sheet\n\n### Column Structure\n- **Column A**: Row labels (GDP, C, I, G, X, M, etc.)\n- **Column B**: Units and descriptions\n- **Column C**: 2024 or latest actual year\n- **Column J**: 2026 (start of projection)\n- **Columns K-Q**: 2027-2033 (investment period)\n- **Columns R-V**: 2034-2043 (post-investment period)\n\n### Key Rows to Calculate\n\n```\nRow for Investment:\n= (Baseline_Investment_USD × Exchange_Rate × Deflator) / Initial_Deflator\n\nRow for Import_Content of Investment:\n= Investment_Value × Import_Content_Share\n\nRow for GDP_Impact:\n= (Investment × Multiplier)\n\nRow for Total_Imports:\n= Baseline_Imports + Import_Content_Impact\n\nRow for GDP:\n= C + I + G + (X - M)\n```\n\n## Assumptions Row (Cells D30-D33)\n\n### Required Entries\n- **D30**: Exchange Rate = 2.746 (Lari/USD, fixed)\n- **D31**: Demand Multiplier = 0.8 (baseline), varies by scenario\n- **D32**: Investment Profile = \"Bell-shaped\" (text descriptor)\n- **D33**: Import Content Share = (linked to C46 from SUT_Calc)\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Link to Data**: Always use formulas linking to WEO_Data and SUT_Calc sheets\n2. **Keep Formulas Visible**: Use Excel Formula Auditing to verify chains\n3. **Scenario Comparison**: Use color coding or clear labels for scenarios\n4. **Validation**: Ensure Y = C + I + G + (X - M) for each year\n5. **Documentation**: Add notes on assumptions and shock timing\n6. **Sensitivity Analysis**: Pre-build scenarios with different parameters\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Unit mismatches**: Ensure all values in same currency/units\n- **Deflator application**: Apply consistently to nominal values\n- **Multiplier timing**: Effects should peak when investment spending is highest\n- **Baseline shifts**: Don't assume baseline remains constant (use WEO projections)\n- **Import leakage**: Higher import content = lower multiplier effect\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":920,"cache_read_input_tokens":19963,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":920,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0e7df011-350c-4ba1-a91f-e7f41ee03588","uuid":"bd882f47-b9e3-4ea1-b9ee-17300a1f886f"}

M)\n```\n\n## Assumptions Row (Cells D30-D33)\n\n### Required Entries\n- **D30**: Exchange Rate = 2.746 (Lari/USD, fixed)\n- **D31**: Demand Multiplier = 0.8 (baseline), varies by scenario\n- **D32**: Investment Profile = \"Bell-shaped\" (text descriptor)\n- **D33**: Import Content Share = (linked to C46 from SUT_Calc)\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Link to Data**: Always use formulas linking to WEO_Data and SUT_Calc sheets\n2. **Keep Formulas Visible**: Use Excel Formula Auditing to verify chains\n3. **Scenario Comparison**: Use color coding or clear labels for scenarios\n4. **Validation**: Ensure Y = C + I + G + (X - M) for each year\n5. **Documentation**: Add notes on assumptions and shock timing\n6. **Sensitivity Analysis**: Pre-build scenarios with different parameters\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Unit mismatches**: Ensure all values in same currency/units\n- **Deflator application**: Apply consistently to nominal values\n- **Multiplier timing**: Effects should peak when investment spending is highest\n- **Baseline shifts**: Don't assume baseline remains constant (use WEO projections)\n- **Import leakage**: Higher import content = lower multiplier effect\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Excel Workflow for Macroeconomic Accounting Analysis\n\n## Overview\nThis skill documents the complete workflow for building, testing, and validating macroeconomic accounting frameworks entirely within Excel, without hardcoding values.\n\n## File Structure: test - demand.xlsx\n\n### Sheet Organization\n\n1. **WEO_Data** (Historical + Projected)\n   - IMF World Economic Outlook data\n   - Rows: GDP, Real GDP Growth, GDP Deflator, Exports, Imports, etc.\n   - Columns: Years 2018-2043\n   - All future values calculated with formulas\n\n2. **SUPPLY** (Original from Geostat)\n   - 38x38 Product x Industry matrix\n   - Original sheet name preserved\n   - No calculations here - reference only\n\n3. **USE** (Original from Geostat)\n   - 38x38 Product x Industry + Final Demand\n   - Original sheet name preserved\n   - No calculations here - reference only\n\n4. **SUT_Calc** (Derived Calculations)\n   - Links to SUPPLY and USE sheets\n   - Column C: Domestic Supply (linked from SUPPLY)\n   - Column D: Imports (linked from USE)\n   - Column E: Total Supply\n   - Columns F-H: Additional metrics\n   - Row 46: Import Content Share (final calculated cell)\n\n5. **NA** (National Accounts - Main Output)\n   - Scenario 1: Baseline (Multiplier=0.8)\n   - Scenario 2: Alternative (Multiplier=1.0)\n   - Scenario 3: Alternative (Import Content=0.5)\n   - Rows: GDP components (C, I, G, X, M, Y)\n   - Columns: Years 2024-2043\n\n## Formula Architecture\n\n### Principle: Chain of Linked Formulas\nEvery calculated cell should trace back to source data:\n\n```\nScenario_GDP\n  ↑\nImpact_on_Imports\n  ↑\nInvestment_Shock × Multiplier\n  ↑\nAnnual_Investment (bell-shaped)\n  ↑\nTotal_Investment × Year_Share\n  ↑\nWEO_Data + Exchange_Rate + SUT_Calc\n```\n\n### No Hardcoded Values in:\n- Future GDP (use WEO growth rates)\n- Future deflators (use calculated growth)\n- Investment values (use bell-shaped formula)\n- Multiplier effects (use assumed parameters)\n- Import content (use SUT calculation)\n\n## Step-by-Step Implementation\n\n### Step 1: WEO_Data Sheet\n\n#### Collect Historical Data (2018-2024)\n- Real GDP Growth Rate (from IMF WEO)\n- GDP Deflator (from IMF WEO)\n- Other components\n\n#### Project Forward (2025-2043)\n```\nCell D30:\n=C30*(1+Growth_Rate)\n\nFor GDP Deflator with fixed growth:\nGrowth_Rate = AVERAGE(Historical_4_Years_Growth)\n=C30*(1+$Fixed_Growth_Rate)\n```\n\n### Step 2: SUT_Calc Sheet\n\n#### Link to Source Tables\n```\nColumn C (Domestic Supply):\n=SUPPLY!C5  (example: pulling from specific cell)\n\nColumn D (Imports):\n=USE!D5\n\nColumn E (Total Supply):\n=C5+D5\n```\n\n#### Calculate Import Content Share (Row 46)\n```\n=SUM(D:D) / SUM(E:E)\nor more precisely:\n=SUM(Range_of_Imports) / SUM(Range_of_Total_Supply)\n```\n\n### Step 3: NA Sheet - Scenario 1\n\n#### Structure\n```\nRow 1-5: Headers and period information\nRow 7-20: Baseline data (linked from WEO_Data)\nRow 22-45: Investment shock and impacts\nRow 30-33: Assumptions\nRow 47-75: Scenario 2\nRow 77-110: Scenario 3\n```\n\n#### Key Formulas\n\n**Investment Annual Value (Bell-shaped)**\n```\nAssume investment 2026-2033 (8 years)\nMid-point = 4.5\n\nAnnual_Share_Year_1 = (1 - ABS(0.5 - 4.5) / 4)^2\nAnnual_Share_Year_2 = (1 - ABS(1.5 - 4.5) / 4)^2\n...\nAnnual_Share_Year_8 = (1 - ABS(8.5 - 4.5) / 4)^2\n\nAnnual_Investment_USD = 6500 × Year_Share_Percent\nAnnual_Investment_Lari = Annual_Investment_USD × 2.746\nAnnual_Investment_Nominal = Annual_Investment_Lari × GDP_Deflator_Index\n```\n\n**GDP Impact Calculation**\n```\nGDP_Impact = (Investment × Multiplier) × Import_Adjustment\n\nWhere:\nImport_Adjustment = 1 - Import_Content_Share\n```\n\n**Scenario Differentials**\n```\nScenario 1: Multiplier = 0.8, Import_Share = SUT_Value\nScenario 2: Multiplier = 1.0, Import_Share = SUT_Value\nScenario 3: Multiplier = 0.8, Import_Share = 0.5\n```\n\n## Validation Checklist\n\n### National Accounting Identity\nFor each year: `GDP = C + I + G + X - M`\n\n```\nExcel Formula:\n=C_Row + I_Row + G_Row + X_Row - M_Row\n\nShould equal GDP_Row (within rounding)\n```\n\n### Shock Timing\n- Investment values peak in middle years\n- Zero investment outside 2026-2033 period\n- Sum of all years = $6.5 billion\n\n### Data Consistency\n- All years reference correct WEO_Data\n- Exchange rate constant at 2.746\n- Import content used in all scenarios correctly\n- Multiplier values match scenario definitions\n\n## Best Practices for Excel Formulas\n\n### Cell Referencing\n- Use **Absolute references** for constants: `$D$31` (multiplier)\n- Use **Mixed references** for growth rates: `$B5` (same column, variable row)\n- Use **Relative references** for time series: `C5` (copy down)\n\n### Named Ranges (Optional but Helpful)\n```\nMultiplier_Baseline = 0.8\nMultiplier_Alt = 1.0\nExchange_Rate = 2.746\nImport_Content_Share_SUT = SUT_Calc!C46\nImport_Content_Share_Alt = 0.5\n```\n\n### Error Checking\n- Validate deflators are > 1 (monotonically increasing)\n- Check investment values are positive\n- Verify GDP grows in absence of shock\n- Confirm imports rise with investment shock\n\n## Output and Reporting\n\n### Scenario Comparison Table\nCreate summary showing:\n- Baseline GDP impact\n- Alternative scenario GDP impacts\n- Difference between scenarios\n- Multiplier effects by year\n\n### Charts (In Excel)\n- Investment profile over time (all scenarios)\n- GDP impact comparison\n- Cumulative GDP effect\n- Import content impact\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n**Issue**: Circular reference error\n- **Solution**: Check investment calculations don't reference own output\n\n**Issue**: Deflator suddenly drops\n- **Solution**: Verify AVERAGE formula used for growth rate, not single year\n\n**Issue**: National accounting identity doesn't balance\n- **Solution**: 2018 < len(historical_gdp):\n            ws_weo.cell(row=4, column=col_idx).value = historical_gdp[year - 2018]\n    else:\n        # Projection formula: Previous Year * (1 + growth rate)\n        # Growth rate: 2025 = 7.2%, then converge to 5% by 2030\n        if year == 2026:\n            ws_weo.cell(row=4, column=col_idx).value = f\"={get_column_letter(col_idx-1)}4*1.072\"\n        elif year <= 2030:\n            # Gradual convergence from 7.2% to 5%\n            convergence_factor = 0.072 - (0.072 - 0.05) * (year - 2026) / 4\n            ws_weo.cell(row=4, column=col_idx).value = f\"={get_column_letter(col_idx-1)}4*(1+{convergence_factor:.4f})\"\n        else:\n            # Steady state at 5%\n            ws_weo.cell(row=4, column=col_idx).value = f\"={get_column_letter(col_idx-1)}4*1.05\"\n\n# Real GDP Growth Rate (%)\nws_weo['A5'] = \"Real GDP Growth Rate (%)\"\ngrowth_rates = [4.6, 4.3, 6.2, -6.8, 10.1, 1.9, 6.6, 9.4, 7.2]  # 2018-2025 (last is projection)\nfor idx, year in enumerate(years):\n    col_idx = idx + 3\n    if year <= 2025:\n        if year - 2026=1, 2027=2, ..., 2033=8\n        mid_point = 4.5\n        weight = (1 - abs(year_position - mid_point) / 4) ** 2\n        bell_weights.append(weight)\n    else:\n        bell_weights.append(0)\n\ntotal_weight = sum(bell_weights)\nbell_weights = [w / total_weight if total_weight > 0 else 0 for w in bell_weights]\n\n# ===== PAGE SETUP =====\nws_na['A1'] = \"NATIONAL ACCOUNTS - INVESTMENT SHOCK ANALYSIS\"\nws_na['A1'].font = Font(bold=True, size=14)\nws_na.merge_cells('A1:K1')\n\nws_na['A2'] = \"Georgia - Demand-side macro accounting framework\"\nws_na.merge_cells('A2:K2')\n\n# Investment parameters\nws_na['A4'] = \"INVESTMENT SHOCK PARAMETERS\"\nws_na['A4'].font = Font(bold=True, size=11)\nws_na['A5'] = \"Total Investment\"\nws_na['C5'] = 6500\nws_na['D5'] = \"USD millions\"\nws_na['A6'] = \"Duration\"\nws_na['C6'] = \"2026-2033\"\nws_na['D6'] = \"(8 years)\"\nws_na['A7'] = \"Profile\"\nws_na['C7'] = \"Bell-shaped\"\n\n# Common assumptions\nws_na['A9'] = \"ASSUMPTIONS\"\nws_na['A9'].font = Font(bold=True, size=11)\nws_na['A10'] = \"Exchange Rate\"\nws_na['D10'] = 2.746\nws_na['E10'] = \"GEL/USD (fixed)\"\nws_na['A11'] = \"Investment Deflator\"\nws_na['D11'] = \"GDP Deflator\"\n\n# Now create the three scenarios\n# Each scenario will have the same table structure repeated 3 times\n\ndef create_scenario(ws, start_row, scenario_num, multiplier, import_share, scenario_name):\n    \"\"\"Helper function to create one scenario table\"\"\"\n    \n    # GEL\"\n    for col_idx, year in enumerate(years):\n        col_letter = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n        prev_col = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n        weo_col = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n        # Investment USD * Exchange rate * (Deflator / Base Deflator)\n        ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = f\"={prev_col}{baseline_inv_row}*$D$10*(WEO_Data!{weo_col}6/$C$6)\"\n        ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'].number_format = '0.0'\n    row += 1\n    \n    # Row 4: Import content of investment\n    import_inv_row = row\n    ws[f'A{row}'] = \"4. GEL\"\n    for col_idx, year in enumerate(years):\n        col_letter = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n        # Investment * import share\n        multiplier_ref = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3) if col_idx == 0 else col_letter\n        if isinstance(import_share, str):\n            ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = f\"={col_letter}{investment_gel_row}*({import_share})\"\n        else:\n            ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = f\"={col_letter}{investment_gel_row}*{import_share}\"\n        ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'].number_format = '0.0'\n    row += 1\n    \n    # Row 5: Domestic demand for investment\n    dom_demand_row = row\n    ws[f'A{row}'] = \"5. Domestic demand for investment\"\n    ws[f'B{row}'] = \"mil. GEL\"\n    for col_idx, year in enumerate(years):\n        col_letter = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n        # Investment - import content = Investment * (1 - import share)\n        if isinstance(import_share, str):\n            ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = f\"={col_letter}{investment_gel_row}*(1-({import_share}))\"\n        else:\n            ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = f\"={col_letter}{investment_gel_row}*(1-{import_share})\"\n        ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'].number_format = '0.0'\n    row += 1\n    \n    # Row 6: GDP impact (multiplier effect)\n    gdp_impact_row = row\n    ws[f'A{row}'] = \"6. GDP impact (multiplier effect)\"\n    ws[f'B{row}'] = \"mil. GEL\"\n    mult_assumption_row = start_row + 2  # Row with the multiplier assumption\n    for col_idx, year in enumerate(years):\n        col_letter = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n        ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = f\"={col_letter}{dom_demand_row}*$D${mult_assumption_row}\"\n        ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'].number_format = '0.0'\n    row += 1\n    \n    # Row 7: Total imports (baseline + import shock)\n    total_imp_row = row\n    ws[f'A{row}'] = \"7. Total imports impact\"\n    ws[f'B{row}'] = \"mil. GEL\"\n    for col_idx, year in enumerate(years):\n        col_letter = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n        # = Import shock + GDP impact * import content (induced imports)\n        ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = f\"={col_letter}{import_inv_row}+{col_letter}{gdp_impact_row}*({import_share if isinstance(import_share, str) else import_share})\"\n        ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'].number_format = '0.0'\n    row += 1\n    \n    # Row 8: GDP with shock\n    gdp_shock_row = row\n    ws[f'A{row}'] = \"8. GEL\"\n    for col_idx, year in enumerate(years):\n        col_letter = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n        ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = f\"={col_letter}{baseline_inv_row - 1}+{col_letter}{gdp_impact_row}\"\n        ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'].number_format = '0.0'\n    row += 1\n    \n    # Row 9: Additional GDP growth (%)\n    ws[f'A{row}'] = \"9. Additional GDP growth\"\n    ws[f'B{row}'] = \"% points\"\n    for col_idx, year in enumerate(years):\n        col_letter = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n        if col_idx == 0:\n            ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = 0\n        else:\n            prev_col = get_column_letter(col_idx + 2)\n            # (GDP_shock - GDP_baseline) / GDP_baseline\n            ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = f\"=({col_letter}{gdp_shock_row}-{col_letter}{baseline_inv_row - 1})/{col_letter}{baseline_inv_row - 1}\"\n            ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'].number_format = '0.00%'\n    row += 1\n    \n    # Row 10: Cumulative GDP effect\n    ws[f'A{row}'] = \"10. GEL\"\n    for col_idx, year in enumerate(years):\n        col_letter = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n        if col_idx == 0:\n            ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = 0\n        else:\n            prev_col = get_column_letter(col_idx + 2)\n            ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'] = f\"={prev_col}{row}+{col_letter}{gdp_impact_row}\"\n            ws[f'{col_letter}{row}'].number_format = '0.0'\n    \n    return row + 2  # Return next available row\n\n# Create the three scenarios\ncurrent_row = 13\n\ncurrent_row = create_scenario(ws_na, current_row, 1, 0.8, \"=SUT_Calc!C46\", \n                               \"SCENARIO 1: BASELINE (Multiplier=0.8, Import Share=SUT)\")\n\ncurrent_row = create_scenario(ws_na, current_row, 2, 1.0, \"=SUT_Calc!C46\",\n                               \"SCENARIO 2: ALTERNATIVE (Multiplier=1.0, Import Share=SUT)\")\n\ncurrent_row = create_scenario(ws_na, current_row, 3, 0.8, 0.5,\n                               \"SCENARIO 3: ALTERNATIVE (Multiplier=0.8, Import Share=0.5)\")\n\n# Set column widths\nws_na.column_dimensions['A'].width = 40\nws_na.column_dimensions['B'].width = 18\nfor col_idx in range(len(years)):\n    col_letter = get_column_letter(col_idx + 3)\n    ws_na.column_dimensions[col_letter].width = 11\n\nwb.save('/root/test - demand.xlsx')\nprint(\"✓ National Accounts sheet rebuilt with proper structure\")\nprint(f\"✓ Total of {len(years)} years: {years[0]}-{years[-1]}\")\nprint(f\"✓ Investment weights verified (sum should be ~1.0): {sum(bell_weights):.4f}\")\n\nEOF\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":370,"cache_read_input_tokens":58454,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":370,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0e7df011-350c-4ba1-a91f-e7f41ee03588","uuid":"f8bf35c4-e895-42ac-8f8d-1974c1c0f8c0"}
